What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because I just keep getting these nagging thoughts. I'm a designer, and I constantly worry that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or that I've sent the wrong version of a file to a client. And the thing is, even when I check and I can see it’s probably fine... my mind just keeps circling back to it. It's getting really frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or checks to try and get rid of these nagging thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I submit any work now, I'll usually... I'll re-open the file just to be sure. I end up comparing the layers, re-saving it, and sometimes I even just sit there and mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. \n\nIt’s not like it takes over my entire day or anything, but... it definitely makes me slower. I just feel a lot less free creatively because I'm always stopping to do all this extra checking.\n\nDoctor: Are these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with others, such as friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my colleagues might notice that I take a little longer to hand in my final files, but it hasn't caused any actual friction or anything. And with my friends and family... no, I don't think it affects them at all. They probably don't even know I do it. It's really just something that happens when I'm working, so I mostly just deal with it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these nagging thoughts or a similar feeling of worry when you're not working on a design project?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. It seems to really just be tied to my work. When I'm... you know, just doing things around the house or out on the weekends, I don't really get that same nagging feeling. It's pretty much just focused on my design files and making sure I didn't mess up a project. Outside of that, I'm generally fine.\n\nDoctor: How distressing do you find these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say it's severely distressing, really. It's more... just annoying. It frustrates me because I know it slows me down and kind of takes the fun out of my work, but it doesn't make me panic or anything like that. It's mostly just a bother that I wish I didn't have to deal with.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of it. It feels like it's been a little while now, but... yeah, I couldn't give you an exact timeframe or tell you exactly when it first started. It just kind of gradually became a habit, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like tension or restlessness when these nagging thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really get any physical symptoms. I don't feel tense or restless or anything like that. It's really just the thoughts in my head, telling me to check things again. My body feels fine.\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts about your design work been constant since they started, or have they come and gone over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really noticed a clear pattern, to be honest. I guess... maybe they come and go a bit depending on what I'm working on? Or they might just always be there in the background. I just haven't really paid close enough attention to know for sure if there are times when it completely stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had to turn down a project or opportunity at work because of these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually turned down any projects. I mean, I’m still getting my work done. But... I have noticed that I've started missing some internal deadlines because of all the checking. And, um, I guess I'm definitely holding back on bolder design ideas lately. I just get afraid that if I try something too ambitious, I’ll overlook something important. I really want to move toward a creative director role, and I'm worried that making a mistake will damage my chances of getting there. So I just kind of play it safe.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to advance in your career as a designer because of these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it is making it harder. I'm a designer, and I constantly worry that I’ve missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or that I've sent the wrong version of a file to a client. And the thing is, even when I check and I can see it’s probably fine... my mind just keeps circling back to it. It's getting really frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or checks to try and get rid of these nagging thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I submit any work now, I'll usually... I'll re-open the file just to be sure. I end up comparing the layers, re-saving it, and sometimes I even just sit there and mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. \n\nIt’s not like it takes over my entire day or anything, but... it definitely makes me slower. I just feel a lot less free creatively because I'm always stopping to do all this extra checking.\n\nDoctor: Are these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with others, such as friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my colleagues might notice that I take a little longer to hand in my final files, but it hasn't caused any actual friction or anything. And with my friends and family... no, I don't think it affects them at all. They probably don't even know I do it. It's really just something that happens when I'm working, so I mostly just deal with it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these nagging thoughts or a similar feeling of worry when you're not working on a design project?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. It seems to really just be tied to my work. When I'm... you know, just doing things around the house or out on the weekends, I don't really get that same nagging feeling. It's pretty much just focused on my design files and making sure I didn't mess up a project. Outside of that, I'm generally fine.\n\nDoctor: How distressing do you find these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say it's severely distressing, really. It's more... just annoying. It frustrates me because I know it slows me down and kind of takes the fun out of my work, but it doesn't make me panic or anything like that. It's mostly just a bother that I wish I didn't have to deal with.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I really want to move toward a creative director role, and I'm worried that making a mistake will damage my chances of getting there. It's really just something that happens when I'm working, so I mostly just deal with it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these nagging thoughts or a similar feeling of worry when you're not working on a design project?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. It seems to really just be tied to my work. When I'm... you know, just doing things around the house or out on the weekends, I don't really get that same nagging feeling. It's pretty much just focused on my design files and making sure I didn't mess up a project. Outside of that, I'm generally fine.\n\nDoctor: How distressing do you find these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say it's severely distressing, really. It's more... just annoying. It frustrates me because I know it slows me down and kind of takes the fun out of my work, but it doesn't make me panic or anything like that. It's mostly just a bother that I wish I didn't have to deal with.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of it. It feels like it's been a little while now, but... yeah, I couldn't give you an exact timeframe or tell you exactly when it first started. It just kind of gradually became a habit, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like tension or restlessness when these nagging thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really get any physical symptoms. I don't feel tense or restless or anything like that. It's really just the thoughts in my head, telling me to check things again. My body feels fine.\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts about your design work been constant since they started, or have they come and gone over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really noticed a clear pattern, to be honest. I guess... maybe they come and go a bit depending on what I'm working on? Or they might just always be there in the background. I just haven't really paid close enough attention to know for sure if there are times when it completely stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had to turn down a project or opportunity at work because of these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually turned down any projects. I mean, I’m still getting my work done. But... I have noticed that I've started missing some internal deadlines because of all the checking. And, um, I guess I'm definitely holding back on bolder design ideas lately. I just get afraid that if I try something too ambitious, I’ll overlook something important. I really want to move toward a creative director role, and I'm worried that making a mistake will damage my chances of getting there. So I just kind of play it safe.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to advance in your career as a designer because of these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it is making it harder. Like I mentioned, I'm still getting my actual work done, but... well, missing those internal deadlines isn't a great look. And because I'm so worried about making a mistake and missing something, I'm definitely holding back on pitching bolder design ideas. I just play it safe. I really want to move up to a creative director role, but I feel like if I keep holding back and stressing over every little detail, it's going to hurt my chances of actually getting there.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are significantly interfering with your ability to perform your job as a designer?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it comes back to what I was just saying. I wouldn't say it's completely stopping me, because I am still getting my actual work done. But it is definitely interfering, yeah. Missing those internal deadlines is becoming a real issue. And I'm just holding back on any of my bolder design ideas now. I'm just so afraid that I'll overlook something important and completely damage my chances of moving toward that creative director role. I mean, everyone has normal worries, like paying bills or whatever, but the constant checking and the nagging thoughts... that's really just focused on my work. It's the design files and the details there that get me stuck. I don't have those same kinds of obsessive worries about other parts of my life.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the distress from these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's mild. It's frustrating, and it definitely gets in my way, like I said, but it's not completely taking over my life or stopping me from functioning. It's just a constant, annoying pressure when I'm working.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are causing you significant emotional distress?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's significant, no. I definitely get stressed out when I'm stuck checking a file over and over, but I'm not breaking down or feeling completely overwhelmed emotionally. It's mostly just this constant annoyance that I wish I could turn off.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are affecting your relationships with colleagues or impacting your overall job performance in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still getting my work done, but... it is starting to have an impact, yeah. I just get so worried that if I try something new, I'll overlook a detail and make a mistake. And with my friends and family... no, I don't think it affects them at all. They probably don't even know I do it. It's really just something that happens when I'm working, so I mostly just deal with it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these nagging thoughts or a similar feeling of worry when you're not working on a design project?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. It seems to really just be tied to my work. When I'm... you know, just doing things around the house or out on the weekends, I don't really get that same nagging feeling. It's pretty much just focused on my design files and making sure I didn't mess up a project. Outside of that, I'm generally fine.\n\nDoctor: How distressing do you find these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say it's severely distressing, really. It's more... just annoying. It frustrates me because I know it slows me down and kind of takes the fun out of my work, but it doesn't make me panic or anything like that. It's mostly just a bother that I wish I didn't have to deal with.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these nagging thoughts about your design work?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of it. It feels like it's been a little while now, but... yeah, I couldn't give you an exact timeframe or tell you exactly when it first started. It just kind of gradually became a habit, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical symptoms like tension or restlessness when these nagging thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really get any physical symptoms. I don't feel tense or restless or anything like that. It's really just the thoughts in my head, telling me to check things again. My body feels fine.\n\nDoctor: Have these nagging thoughts about your design work been constant since they started, or have they come and gone over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really noticed a clear pattern, to be honest. I guess... maybe they come and go a bit depending on what I'm working on? Or they might just always be there in the background. I just haven't really paid close enough attention to know for sure if there are times when it completely stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had to turn down a project or opportunity at work because of these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually turned down any projects. I mean, I’m still getting my work done. But... I have noticed that I've started missing some internal deadlines because of all the checking. And, um, I guess I'm definitely holding back on bolder design ideas lately. I just get afraid that if I try something too ambitious, I’ll overlook something important. I really want to move toward a creative director role, and I'm worried that making a mistake will damage my chances of getting there. So I just kind of play it safe.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to advance in your career as a designer because of these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it is making it harder.
